# Appendix C: LoRA banks and RegMean - tables generated from the raw result files

Generated by `make_tables.py` on 2026-10-18 23:22 (local time) from the files listed in X4; nothing in the tables is typed by hand except the 'doc value' column of X1. Source paths are written `master:results/...` (= /data/home/azpitarte/condmerge/results/...) and `aa:results/aa/...` (= /data/home/azpitarte/condmerge_aa/results/aa/..., branch aa/side).


### General legend

- **Experts / splits.** 8 CLIP ViT-B/32 task experts: sun397, cars, resisc45, eurosat, svhn, gtsrb, mnist, dtd. `dev` = 20% of the official test set (model selection), `test` = the other 80% (frozen configs only).
- **Information budgets.** A = weights only; B = pooled unlabeled inputs without task IDs; C = task-separated unlabeled inputs (expert i sees task i's unlabeled calibration images); D = labeled dev data (oracle / diagnostic only). LoRA RegMean names: `C` (= budget C), `Bp` (= budget B, pooled through each expert), `B0` (pooled through the base model; RegMean must equal averaging).
- **Metrics.** LoRA files (`aa:`): `avg_acc` = ABSOLUTE accuracy, mean over the 8 tasks of correct/total (not normalized); shown in percent. Master full-FT files (`master:`): `mean_norm` = NORMALIZED accuracy (per task accuracy / that task's own expert accuracy, averaged), `mean_acc` = absolute accuracy, `min_norm` = worst task's normalized accuracy; shown in percent. Differences are in percentage points (pt). The two sides are never mixed in one column; C5 is normalized-and-absolute, C1-C4 and C6 are absolute.
- **The rule.** lambda_i = softmax_i(-beta * kappa2(W_i)), beta = b / (median over maps of the across-expert std of the score); merge W0 + alpha * sum_i lambda_i (W_i - W0). b = 0 is uniform, b = inf is argmin selection.
- **Banks.** `knots_release` (the KnOTS-release rank-16 bank, also called `knots_lora_r16_vitb32`), `lora_c5a` (KnOTS recipe, seed 0), `lora_c5a_seed1` (same recipe, seed 1), `lora` (= vit20, 2000 steps). Only the KnOTS release bank has spectra (C2) and the rule sweep (C3).
- **RegMean.** D (G + rho I) = sum_i D_i G~_i + rho R, G~ = a G + (1 - a) diag G; 'kappa cap' = smallest rho with kappa2(G + rho I) <= kappa_t; `rel_ridge` = fixed rho = c tr(G)/d.
- **Number formats.** Percent with 2 decimals unless stated; kappa and tiny quantities in scientific notation; '-' = not applicable / not recorded; 'inf' = numerically singular.

## C1. LoRA exactness and gauge checks (KnOTS release bank, rank 16)

**What is varied / split / metric / units.** Nothing is tuned here: each row is an algebraic or numerical check on the 8 KnOTS rank-16 adapters (48 attention maps = 12 blocks x {q,k,v,out}_proj, 8 experts, 384 (map, expert) pairs; gamma = 1); no split and no accuracy (spectral quantities only); values are unitless relative errors, ratios or counts.

**Source:** `aa:results/aa/lora/lora_checks.json`; `aa:results/aa/lora/lora_stats.csv (for the rows marked 'recomputed from lora_stats.csv')`; `aa:scripts/aa_lora_spectra.py (the script that wrote both files), aa:tests/test_aa_lora.py`


### C1a. All checks

*What / split / metric / units.* Varied: nothing (a list of checks); split: none; metric: relative errors, ratios, counts; units: unitless.

| interlacing_violations | 0 | count of (map, expert) with sigma_q(W0+Delta) > sigma_{q-r}(W0); criterion == 0 (of 384) | PASS |

'PASS' thresholds are set in this generator (1e-8 for the core check, 1e-9 for the gauge checks); the peer's files carry no thresholds.


## C2. LoRA spectra summary (KnOTS release bank, rank 16)

**What is varied / split / metric / units.** Descriptive statistics of per-(map, expert) spectra over the 4 attention map types (`module` = q_proj, k_proj, v_proj, out_proj; 12 blocks x 8 experts = 96 pairs each); no split and no accuracy; kappa2 and sigma values are unitless / weight-scale numbers, ranks are counts. NOTE: lora_stats.csv exists for ONE bank only (the KnOTS release, loaded by aa_lora_spectra.py via load_knots_lora); there is no `bank` column and no spectra file for lora_c5a, lora_c5a_seed1 or lora (vit20).

## C3. LoRA literal-rule sweep (KnOTS release bank), dev

**What is varied / split / metric / units.** Varied: the score rule (`label`), the temperature multiplier `b` (beta = b / median-across-maps std of the rule's scores; b = 0 is uniform, b = inf is argmin selection) and the update scale `alpha` (merge W0 + alpha * sum_i lambda_i Delta_i on the 48 adapted maps); split = dev (20% of the official test set); budget A (weights only); metric = `avg_acc`, the ABSOLUTE accuracy averaged over the 8 tasks (verified: avg_acc == mean of the per-task `acc` in every row, max abs diff 0.0e+00); units = percent (x100).

**Source:** `aa:results/aa/lora/rule_sweep_dev_shard0.jsonl`; `aa:results/aa/lora/rule_sweep_dev_shard1.jsonl`; `aa:results/aa/lora/rule_sweep_dev_shard2.jsonl`; `aa:scripts/aa_lora_rule_sweep.py`

Data notes: bank = `knots_lora_r16_vitb32` in all 556 raw rows (this sweep exists for the KnOTS release bank only; other banks appear in C4b/C4f); split `dev`, budget `A`; rows per shard file {'rule_sweep_dev_shard0.jsonl': 227, 'rule_sweep_dev_shard1.jsonl': 230, 'rule_sweep_dev_shard2.jsonl': 99}; 18 configurations appear in two shards (identical `chash`) - 0 of them with different accuracy; after removing the duplicates 538 unique configurations remain, and all tables below use these. The grid is ragged: the first launch used the full-FT alpha grid (kept in the shard files). Labels with alpha above 1.6: `kappa`, `kappa_rev`, `metagpt`, `single:cars`, `single:dtd`, `single:eurosat`, `single:gtsrb`, `single:mnist`, `single:resisc45`, `single:sun397`, `single:svhn`, `uniform` (alpha up to 3.2); all other labels have alpha in {0.6, 0.8, 1, 1.3, 1.6}.

Rules defined in `aa_lora_rule_sweep.py` but with NO row in the dev shards: `dlogkplus_0.001`, `dlogkplus_0.001_rev`, `dstable_rank`, `dentropy_rank`, `factor_logkappaA`, `factor_logkappaA_gauged`, `global:logkappa`, `uniform_rank16`. (`uniform_rank16` and the frozen shuffles at b = 128 are evaluated on test in C4g.)

Legend: `kappa` = softmax(-beta * kappa2(W_i)) per map (the literal rule), `kappa_rev` = +beta (heaviest weight on the largest kappa), `logkappa` = on log kappa2, `kappa_z` = on the per-map z-scored kappa, `shufK:kappa` = the same scores permuted across experts within each map (seed K; matched-concentration null), `global:kappa` = one weight vector per expert from the mean score, `dnorm` = on the Frobenius norm of Delta (magnitude control), `dlogkplus_0.01` = log kappa_plus(Delta; tau = 0.01), `metagpt` = lambda proportional to ||Delta_i||^2 summed over all maps, `single:<task>` = one-hot on that expert for every map, `uniform` = lambda = 1/8 (Task Arithmetic with scale alpha). The `_rev` variants flip the sign of the score.


## C4. LoRA selection analyses

**What is varied / split / metric / units.** Varied (per sub-table): C4a/C4b the selection scheme (argmin-kappa vs count-preserving, free random, global-frequency selections; 4 banks), C4c gentlest/strongest hard selections, C4d the oracle allocation vs spectral scores, C4e single-map hand-overs, C4f the transfer of master's rule `logkappa_q90_rev`, C4g frozen test configurations; split = dev unless a sub-table says test (test_freeze, oracle test column); metric = absolute `avg_acc` (per-task `acc` averaged, verified per row below) in percent (x100) unless it is a correlation or a rank.

**Source:** `aa:results/aa/lora/selection_controls.jsonl`; `aa:results/aa/lora/selection_controls_null20.jsonl`; `aa:results/aa/lora/replicate_banks.jsonl`; `aa:results/aa/lora/gentle.json`; `aa:results/aa/lora/oracle_vs_scores.json`; `aa:results/aa/lora/oracle_vs_scores.md`; `aa:results/aa/lora/single_map_swap_summary.json`; `aa:results/aa/lora/single_map_swap.jsonl`; `aa:results/aa/lora/q90_transfer.jsonl`; `aa:results/aa/lora/test_freeze.jsonl`


### C4a. Selection controls on the KnOTS release bank (dev, budget A): argmin-kappa vs 5- and 20-seed nulls

*What / split / metric / units.* Varied: selection scheme (kappa_argmin / global_freq / count_preserving / free_random_selection) x alpha x seed; split: dev; metric: avg_acc (absolute); units: percent, percentile of a null.  
*Source:* `aa:results/aa/lora/selection_controls.jsonl`; `aa:results/aa/lora/selection_controls_null20.jsonl`

`kappa_argmin` = per map the expert with the smallest kappa2(W_i) takes the whole map (b = inf); `global_freq` = lambda_i = (# maps won by expert i)/48 on every map; `count_preserving` = the same multiset of 48 per-map winners randomly reassigned across maps (seed s); `free_random_selection` = each map assigned to a uniformly random expert (seed s).

**C4a-1. Every row of `selection_controls.jsonl` (21 rows; 5 seeds; alpha in {0.8, 1, 1.3})**

### C4d. Does the LoRA oracle allocation (budget D) follow any spectral score? (`oracle_vs_scores.json`, `.md`)

*What / split / metric / units.* Varied: oracle (2) x spectral score (7); split: dev for fitting, test for the oracle's own accuracy; metric: Spearman correlations with permutation p-values, avg_acc (absolute); units: unitless / percent.  
*Source:* `aa:results/aa/lora/oracle_vs_scores.json`; `aa:results/aa/lora/oracle_vs_scores.md`

Two oracles: per-layer (per-map) lambda trained on labelled dev data, `layerwise_a1_seed0` at alpha fixed to 1 and `layerwise_alpha_seed0` with alpha also learned. For each score: within-map Spearman between the oracle's lambda and the score (permutation null), between-expert correlation (8 points), and within-expert correlation (shuffle maps per expert). `dev` and `test` columns are the oracle's own absolute avg_acc (the oracle is fitted on dev, so `dev` is optimistic; `test` is the untouched split).

### C4e. Per-map marginal value of handing one map to one expert (dev, uniform base, alpha = 1; `single_map_swap*`)

*What / split / metric / units.* Varied: map (48) x receiving expert (8), one map handed over at a time to a uniform base; split: dev; metric: avg_acc (absolute) and gain over the uniform base; units: percent, pt.  
*Source:* `aa:results/aa/lora/single_map_swap.jsonl`; `aa:results/aa/lora/single_map_swap_summary.json`; `aa:results/aa/lora/lora_stats.csv`

Baseline = uniform lambda on all 48 maps at alpha = 1. For each map p and expert i, only map p is switched to one-hot(i) and the dev `avg_acc` (absolute, mean over tasks) is recorded: 48 x 8 = 384 swaps + 1 baseline = 385 rows. `gain` = swap accuracy - baseline accuracy, in percentage points.

| argmin_kappa_mean_rank (1=best, 8=worst, random 4.5) | 4.270833 | 4.270833 | 0.0e+00 |

| frac_argmin_kappa_is_best | 0.104167 | 0.104167 | 0.0e+00 |

### C4f. Transfer of master's dev-best full-FT rule `logkappa_q90_rev` to the four LoRA banks (dev, budget A; `q90_transfer.jsonl`)

## C5. Full fine-tune RegMean (8 FusionBench CLIP ViT-B/32 full-FT experts), master

**What is varied / split / metric / units.** Varied: the RegMean regulariser (off-diagonal shrinkage a, kappa cap kappa_t on G with ridge target mean/zero, fixed relative ridge), the update scale alpha, the Gram budget (C = task-separated: expert i on its own task's unlabeled train images; B = every expert on the same pooled images, no task ids), the number n of calibration images per task (16/64/512) and the calibration subset seed; split = dev (20% of the official test set) for model selection and test (the other 80%) for frozen configs; metric = `mean_norm` = mean over the 8 tasks of accuracy / accuracy of that task's own expert (NORMALIZED accuracy; expert accuracies from master:results/experts/expert_accuracy.json), `mean_acc` = unweighted mean ABSOLUTE accuracy, `min_norm` = worst task's normalized accuracy; units = percent (x100). Verification that the stored metrics are what they say: see C5i.

**Source:** `master:results/regmean/regmean_C_dev.jsonl`; `master:results/regmean/regmean_B_dev.jsonl`; `master:results/regmean/instability_C_dev.jsonl`; `master:results/regmean/regmean_C_test.jsonl`; `master:results/regmean/regmean_B_test.jsonl`; `master:results/regmean/instability_C_test.jsonl`; `master:results/regmean/twobytwo_C_dev.jsonl`; `master:results/regmean/twobytwo_C_test.jsonl`; `master:results/regmean/twobytwo_summary.json`; `master:results/regmean/strength_sweep_C_dev_n16s0.jsonl`; `master:results/regmean/strength_sweep_C_dev_n16s1.jsonl`; `master:results/regmean/strength_sweep_C_dev_n16s2.jsonl`; `master:results/regmean/strength_sweep_C_dev_n512s0.jsonl`; `master:results/regmean/strength_sweep_C_test_n16s0.jsonl`; `master:results/regmean/strength_sweep_C_test_n16s1.jsonl`; `master:results/regmean/strength_sweep_C_test_n16s2.jsonl`; `master:results/regmean/strength_sweep_C_test_n512s0.jsonl`; `master:src/condmerge/regmean.py, master:experiments/regmean_sweep.py, regmean_instability.py, regmean_2x2.py, analyze_regmean_2x2.py`

Legend: **RegMean** per linear map solves D (G + rho I) = sum_i D_i G~_i + rho R with G = sum_i G~_i, G_i = E_task-i[x x^T] (input second moment of expert i), off-diagonal shrinkage G~_i = a G_i + (1 - a) diag(G_i). `shrink` rows: rho = 0. **kappa cap** (`kcap_mean`, `kcap_zero`; a = 1): rho = the smallest ridge with kappa2(G + rho I) <= kappa_t and R = mean of the updates (`mean`) or 0 (`zero`). Merged weights W0 + alpha * D (alpha = 1 is standard RegMean); non-linear parameters get the uniform average. 2x2 / sweep cells: `kcap_ridge` (kappa-cap ridge toward the mean), `kcap_shrink` (the largest a with kappa2(a G + (1 - a) diag G) <= kappa_t), `fixed_shrink` (a fixed), `rel_ridge` (fixed relative ridge rho = c tr(G)/d, c = r_med / kappa_t with r_med the median over maps of lambda_max/(tr/d), `c_rel` = that c). `rel_rho_median` = median over maps of rho / (tr(G)/d) (the effective relative ridge); `shrink_median` / `shrink_min` = median / min over maps of the shrinkage a used.

Completeness of `strength_sweep_C_dev_*`: files present ['n16s0', 'n16s1', 'n16s2', 'n512s0'] (expected ['n16s0', 'n16s1', 'n16s2', 'n512s0']); rows per file {'strength_sweep_C_dev_n16s0.jsonl': 19, 'strength_sweep_C_dev_n16s1.jsonl': 19, 'strength_sweep_C_dev_n16s2.jsonl': 19, 'strength_sweep_C_dev_n512s0.jsonl': 19}; complete: True. Rows per arm in the first file: {'kcap_ridge': 7, 'rel_ridge': 7, 'fixed_shrink': 5}.

Completeness of `strength_sweep_C_test_*`: files present ['n16s0', 'n16s1', 'n16s2', 'n512s0'] (expected ['n16s0', 'n16s1', 'n16s2', 'n512s0']); rows per file {'strength_sweep_C_test_n16s0.jsonl': 3, 'strength_sweep_C_test_n16s1.jsonl': 3, 'strength_sweep_C_test_n16s2.jsonl': 3, 'strength_sweep_C_test_n512s0.jsonl': 3}; complete: True. Rows per arm in the first file: {'kcap_ridge': 1, 'rel_ridge': 1, 'fixed_shrink': 1}.

*What / split / metric / units.* Varied: calibration images per task n in {16, 64, 512} x subset seed x method x alpha; budget C; split: dev; metric: mean_norm (NORMALIZED), mean_acc, kappa2(G); units: percent / unitless.  
*Source:* `master:results/regmean/instability_C_dev.jsonl`

Budget C, n images per task in {16, 64, 512} (n = 512 is one set, seed 0 = all calibration images; n = 16 and 64 use 3 random subsets, seeds 0/1/2), alpha in {1.0, 1.4}. `method` = `shrink` (a = `shrink`; 1.0 = plain RegMean, 0.0 = diagonal Gram) or `kcap_mean` (kappa cap kappa_t = `kappa_target`, ridge toward the mean). In this dev file the kappa statistics are logged only on the `kcap_mean` rows (kappa(G) of the plain summed Gram, before the cap; after the cap = kappa_t unless kappa(G) < kappa_t); the `shrink` rows have null kappa fields (the test file `instability_C_test.jsonl` logs them on every row, see C5e-3).

### C5g. strength_sweep_C_dev_* and strength_sweep_C_test_*: equal-grid post-hoc sweep, every row and per-arm summary

*What / split / metric / units.* Varied: arm (kappa-cap ridge / fixed relative ridge / fixed shrinkage) x grid value x Gram set; alpha = 1; split: dev (all grid) and test (dev-selected values only); metric: mean_norm (NORMALIZED), mean_acc; units: percent, pt.  
*Source:* `master:results/regmean/strength_sweep_C_dev_n16s0.jsonl`; `master:results/regmean/strength_sweep_C_dev_n16s1.jsonl`; `master:results/regmean/strength_sweep_C_dev_n16s2.jsonl`; `master:results/regmean/strength_sweep_C_dev_n512s0.jsonl`; `master:results/regmean/strength_sweep_C_test_n16s0.jsonl`; `master:results/regmean/strength_sweep_C_test_n16s1.jsonl`; `master:results/regmean/strength_sweep_C_test_n16s2.jsonl`; `master:results/regmean/strength_sweep_C_test_n512s0.jsonl`

Budget C, alpha = {1}. Arms (equal grids): `kcap_ridge` kappa_t in {1000, 3000, 1e4, 3e4, 1e5, 3e5, 1e6}; `rel_ridge` with c = r_med / kappa_t over kappa_t in {1000, 3000, 1e4, 3e4, 1e5, 3e5, 1e6}; `fixed_shrink` a in {0.5, 0.8, 0.9, 0.95, 0.99}. Gram sets n16s0, n16s1, n16s2 (n = 16 images per task, random subsets with seeds 0-2) and n512s0. Declared selection rule (RESEARCH_LOG 22:40/22:45): per arm and per n, the grid value with the highest DEV mean_norm (for n = 16, the mean over the 3 subsets); only those are evaluated on test.

## C6. LoRA RegMean (rank-16 banks): dev sweeps, calibration size, 2x2, frozen test

**What is varied / split / metric / units.** Varied: bank (knots_release, lora_c5a, lora = vit20), Gram budget (C task-separated / Bp pooled label-free through each expert / B0 pooled through the base), solve (full 768-dim input space vs exact adapter subspace `sub`), regulariser (RegMean off-diagonal shrinkage a = `shrink`; kappa cap `kappa_target` with ridge toward `zero` or the `mean` update), alpha in {0.8, 1.0, 1.3}, calibration images per task N in {512, 64, 16} and the N = 16 subset (s0/s1/s2), and rank-16 truncation (`r16`); split = dev for the sweeps and the 2x2, test (N = 512, C, full solve) for `regmean_test.json`; metric = `avg_acc`, the ABSOLUTE accuracy averaged over the 8 tasks (NOT normalized; verified in C6h), units = percent (x100).

**Source:** `aa:results/aa/lora/regmean_knots_release_dev.jsonl`; `aa:results/aa/lora/regmean_knots_release_n64_dev.jsonl`; `aa:results/aa/lora/regmean_knots_release_n16_dev.jsonl`; `aa:results/aa/lora/regmean_knots_release_n16_s1_dev.jsonl`; `aa:results/aa/lora/regmean_knots_release_n16_s2_dev.jsonl`; `aa:results/aa/lora/regmean_knots_release_r16_dev.jsonl`; `aa:results/aa/lora/regmean_lora_c5a_dev.jsonl`; `aa:results/aa/lora/regmean_lora_dev.jsonl`; `aa:results/aa/lora/regmean_knots_release_maps.json`; `aa:results/aa/lora/regmean_knots_release_n64_maps.json`; `aa:results/aa/lora/regmean_knots_release_n16_maps.json`; `aa:results/aa/lora/regmean_knots_release_n16_s1_maps.json`; `aa:results/aa/lora/regmean_knots_release_n16_s2_maps.json`; `aa:results/aa/lora/regmean_knots_release_r16_maps.json`; `aa:results/aa/lora/regmean_lora_c5a_maps.json`; `aa:results/aa/lora/regmean_lora_maps.json`; `aa:results/aa/lora/regmean_2x2_dev.jsonl`; `aa:results/aa/lora/regmean_test.json`; `aa:results/aa/lora/regmean_summary.md`; `aa:scripts/aa_lora_regmean.py, aa_lora_regmean_2x2.py, aa_lora_regmean_test.py, aa_lora_grams.py, aa_summarize_regmean.py; aa:src/condmerge/regmean_lora.py`

Legend: per adapted map (48 attention maps) D = (sum_i D_i G~_i)(sum_i G~_i + rho I)^-1 with G~ = a G + (1 - a) diag G (a = `shrink`; a = 1 plain RegMean). `kappa_target` = kappa cap: rho = smallest ridge with kappa2(sum G~ + rho I) <= kappa_t (`median_rho` = median over maps); `ridge_to` = `zero` (plain Tikhonov) or `mean` (ridge toward the uniform average of the updates). `solve = full`: dense 768x768 solve, output rank <= 128; `solve = sub`: solve restricted to the span P of the experts' A-factors (m <= 128 dims), exact for each expert. Budgets: C = expert i on its own task's calibration split (task-separated, unlabeled); Bp = every expert on the pooled 8 x N images (label-free, task-ID-free); B0 = pooled images through the BASE model (all experts share one Gram, so RegMean must equal averaging; sanity check). `median_kappa_G` / `max_kappa_G` = median / max over maps of kappa2(sum G~) (inf = numerically singular), `median_kappa_solve` = of the matrix actually solved. `uniform` rows = plain average of the dense updates. A collapsed solve returns exactly 4.09% (chance-level constant prediction), shown as 4.09.

Banks with RegMean runs: ['knots_release', 'lora', 'lora_c5a']. Banks present elsewhere in the LoRA results but WITHOUT any RegMean file: ['lora_c5a_seed1'] (directory listing of aa:artifacts/cache/aa: 13 Gram files `grams_lora_*`, none for ['lora_c5a_seed1']).

The N = 16 'seeds': per `aa_lora_grams.py`, `--seed -1` (the default, file suffix `_n16`, labelled `n16_s0` in the 2x2 script) takes the FIRST N calibration images, `--seed k >= 0` a random subset of N (suffix `_n16_s<k>`). So LoRA s0 is a deterministic prefix, while master's full-FT n = 16 subsets (C5d) are random subsets with seeds 0/1/2: the calibration images differ between the two sides.

q_proj, k_proj and v_proj of a block share one input Gram (the LN1 output, `L<l>.attn_in`), so their kappa_G are identical by construction; out_proj has its own (`L<l>.out_in`): the 48 maps contain only 24 distinct Grams. Maps with a numerically singular Gram (kappa_G = inf) in `regmean_knots_release_maps.json` C/full: ['L0.k_proj', 'L0.q_proj', 'L0.v_proj'].


### C6e. regmean_2x2_dev.jsonl: isotropic ridge vs diagonal shrinkage x kappa-adaptive vs fixed strength (KnOTS, C, full, alpha = 0.8, dev)

*What / split / metric / units.* Varied: cell (kappa-cap ridge / kappa-cap shrinkage / fixed shrinkage / fixed relative ridge) x strength x Gram set (n512, n16_s0/s1/s2); alpha = 0.8; KnOTS, C, full; split: dev; metric: avg_acc (ABSOLUTE); units: percent, pt.  
*Source:* `aa:results/aa/lora/regmean_2x2_dev.jsonl`

Cells (`cell`): `kcap_ridge` (isotropic ridge, strength set by the kappa cap `param` = kappa_t), `kcap_shrink` (diagonal shrinkage a, the largest a meeting the cap `param` = kappa_t; `frac_cap_unreachable` = fraction of maps where even a = 0 violates the cap), `fixed_shrink` (`param` = a), `rel_ridge` (fixed relative ridge rho = `param` * tr(G)/d; `param` = c = r / kappa_t with r = the median over maps of lambda_max/(tr/d) of the N = 512 Gram). `grams` = n512 or the three N = 16 sets (n16_s0 = first 16 images, n16_s1, n16_s2 = random subsets). ### C6f. regmean_test.json: frozen TEST evaluation (C budget, N = 512, full solve; dev-best alpha per config frozen)

*What / split / metric / units.* Varied: bank (3) x config (uniform, shrink0.95, kcap1e4_zero) at the dev-best alpha; split: TEST; metric: avg_acc and per-task accuracy (ABSOLUTE) and the paired-bootstrap CI of kcap minus shrink; units: percent, pt.  
*Source:* `aa:results/aa/lora/regmean_test.json`; `aa:results/aa/lora/regmean_knots_release_dev.jsonl`; `aa:results/aa/lora/regmean_lora_c5a_dev.jsonl`; `aa:results/aa/lora/regmean_lora_dev.jsonl`

Configs: `uniform`; `shrink0.95` (RegMean with a = 0.95); `kcap1e4_zero` (kappa cap 1e4, ridge -> zero). Test = the 80% split; metric = absolute `avg_acc` and per-task `acc` (%). `kcap_minus_shrink` = `kcap1e4_zero` minus `shrink0.95` (point estimate and 95% paired bootstrap CI over test images within task, 2000 resamples; example sampling only, not calibration resampling). Alpha is verified below against the dev-best alpha of the same config in the dev file.

### C6h. Verification that every LoRA accuracy is ABSOLUTE accuracy (avg_acc), per file

*What / split / metric / units.* Varied: file; split: as stated; metric: max abs difference between avg_acc and the mean of per-task accuracies, and cross-file agreement of the same uniform merge; units: fraction.  
*Source:* `aa:results/aa/lora/(all LoRA jsonl files above)`

Per-task `acc` is correct/total on that task's split (aa:src/condmerge/aa_common.py `TaskData.evaluate`: `acc[t] = correct / n`; `avg_acc = mean(acc)`); no division by an expert's accuracy anywhere in the LoRA pipeline (unlike master's `mean_norm`). Cross-file consistency of the same nominal configuration (uniform on the KnOTS release bank, dev):

**C6h-2. Cross-file consistency**

| config in file A | A (abs. %) | same config in file B | B (abs. %) | abs diff (fraction) |
|:---|---:|:---|---:|---:|

| rule_sweep uniform a=1.0 | 52.0797 | q90_transfer knots_release uniform a=1.0 | 52.0797 | 0.0e+00 |

Differences of order 1e-5 to 1e-3 between files for the same nominal uniform merge are most likely numerical (the uniform update is built by different code paths: a float32 weighted sum in the sweep scripts, a float64 mean cast to float32 in the RegMean scripts, evaluated in fp16, so a few images flip); this is an inference from the scripts, not something the files record. It is not a different metric: the largest difference above is 3.0e-04 (fraction), i.e. 0.030 pt.
